OPEN_SOURCE is introduced as a classification assigned by the original author. The page then specifies five assumptions that constitute its own definition. These assumptions deserve careful attention because they explain what karbytes means by openness in this document. The term is doing more than indicating that source code can be inspected. It expresses a broad set of permissions concerning reproduction, distribution, modification, authorship claims, and the continued status of released material.

The first permission allows any person to make and retain indefinitely many copies, whether those copies are unchanged or modified. Both actions are included: producing a copy and keeping it. The permission therefore covers continuing possession as well as the initial act of duplication. The inclusion of modified copies means that the author’s policy is not confined to faithful reproduction. It also extends to material derived through alteration.

The second permission allows unrestricted distribution or sale of indefinitely many unchanged or modified copies. This is a substantial part of the author’s stated approach because the possibility of selling copies is included alongside giving them away. Its concern is that the material can continue to move among recipients, regardless of whether a particular transaction involves payment.

That permission also shows that public availability and commercial exchange are not treated as mutually exclusive categories within the page’s model. A recipient may distribute the material freely, while another may sell an instance of it. The original author’s declaration aims to leave those choices open. The third permission allows editing of indefinitely many unchanged or already modified copies. This explicitly accommodates successive stages of transformation. A recipient need not preserve the author’s version as the only permissible state, and a previously modified version can itself become the starting point for further editing. The collection’s released content is therefore understood as reusable material from which additional versions can be produced.

The fourth permission allows any person to claim authorship of indefinitely many unchanged or modified copies. This is one of the document’s most permissive provisions. It means that the author’s stated policy does not demand that every circulating instance preserve an exclusive authorship claim for karbytes. At the same time, the document itself repeatedly identifies karbytes as the original author of the websites and their content. The page then presents a pseudocode thought experiment involving Person_A, Person_B, and two objects called X and Y. Its numbered sequence gives the argument a procedural form. Instead of describing a general disagreement about ownership, the author specifies a succession of actions and the authority assumed to accompany each action. The result is intended to expose contradictions that can arise within the definitions the page has established.

In the first step, Person_A creates an original piece of intellectual property called X. The example assumes that X begins as Person_A’s private property. Under that assumption, Person_A alone has legal authority over how other people obtain or use it, including authority to prohibit others from accessing it. In the second step, Person_A designates X as public domain intellectual property. In the third step, Person_B copies X and gives the copy the name Y. The apparent contradiction arises from associating separate control claims with informationally identical objects. The sequence also raises a question of provenance. Renaming X as Y does not reverse the order in which their contents came into existence. An explanatory distinction helps make the argument’s structure clearer. It defines nature as the sole totality containing all phenomena, all noumena, and all non-existence, then explains how observation, absence, an underlying substrate, and different frames of reference relate within that totality. Its central distinctions are between absolute absence and a background compatible with existence, between observed and unobserved phenomena, and between a limited observer’s momentary perspective and an imagined perspective encompassing everything. The page’s displayed last-updated date is 30 September 2025, and its content is designated public domain intellectual property. Its definitions establish the author’s conceptual vocabulary; its examples and symbolic statements illustrate how that vocabulary is intended to function. [karlina_object](https://karlinaobject.wordpress.com/nature/?utm_source=chatgpt.com)

This makes the page an attempt to describe the relationship between existence and observation at the broadest possible level. A particular object, an individual experience, a numerical representation, an unobserved referent, and a frame of reference all belong within the encompassing account.

This does not mean that every absence is treated as a tangible object. Instead, the framework includes concepts specifying what the absence of phenomena or noumena would mean. The later definitions distinguish ordinary failures to observe something from the much stronger condition of there being nothing whatsoever.

The document begins its conceptual work by explaining a revision in terminology. NONGENERATIVE_VOID has been replaced by EXCLUSIVE_VOID, while GENERATIVE_VOID has been replaced by NONEXCLUSIVE_VOID. The author considers the revised terms logically preferable because they avoid embedding assumptions about causal production in the names of the categories.

The older distinction concerned generation. A generative void suggests that a void produces something, while a nongenerative void suggests that it does not. The author wants the terminology to address a different question: whether a void can coexist with phenomena and noumena. The revision shifts attention from a proposed process of production to the compatibility or incompatibility of conditions.

This shift is consequential. Saying that a background exists alongside phenomena leaves the origin of those phenomena open. Saying that the background generates them introduces a causal relationship. The page’s revised vocabulary allows the author to specify an encompassing condition without making its name itself an account of how particular things come into existence.

The first symbolic statement associates exclusive void with zero and nonexclusive void with infinity. Zero represents a condition in which nothing constitutes all that exists or can ever exist. Infinity represents a condition in which infinitely many things exist or can possibly exist. The analogy compresses the author’s broad contrast between total absence and an unbounded domain of existence or possibility.

The wording on the infinity side includes both actuality and possibility. Infinitely many things actually existing is a stronger claim than infinitely many things being possible. The page groups these ideas within its symbolic contrast without developing a separate argument for each. The analogy establishes the intended breadth of the framework, while leaving some distinctions within that breadth implicit.

EXCLUSIVE_VOID is then defined as an absolutely ubiquitous absence of every phenomenon and every noumenon. Each qualification matters. Absolute absence excludes exceptions. Ubiquity makes the absence encompass the entire domain being described. Including noumena rules out the possibility that something exists beyond observation even though nothing appears within a particular observer’s experience.

The result is a demanding conception of nothingness. A frame of reference failing to detect an object does not satisfy it. Neither does a region lacking some expected feature. Exclusive void would require the absence of observed phenomena and unobserved referents alike. It is the framework’s category for complete exclusion of anything that could appear or remain outside a partial view.

The author argues that reading the web page is sufficient to rule out this condition from the reader’s frame of reference. The page has been observed by at least one frame—identified directly as the reader’s—and therefore at least one phenomenon has occurred. Under the definition, that occurrence contradicts an absolutely ubiquitous absence of phenomena.

The argument’s strength comes from how little it needs. It does not require a comprehensive inventory of reality. One observed occurrence is sufficient to defeat a condition defined by the absence of every occurrence. The document uses its own appearance as the example, making the reader’s engagement with it part of the reasoning.

Acceptance of the page’s broader philosophy is unnecessary for this limited inference. The reader can encounter the page while questioning its claims. The encounter still supplies an occurrence within that frame of reference. The existence of an observation and the correctness of an explanation of that observation are separate matters.

The page describes exclusive void as unconditionally nonexistent according to the observing frame. That formulation combines a strong conclusion with a specified standpoint. Within the definitions, the frame’s observation rules out the total absence being considered. It does not independently establish every additional property the author assigns to the alternative background.

This distinction is important when the zero-and-infinity analogy is considered alongside the argument. NONEXCLUSIVE_VOID supplies the alternative category. It is defined as an unconditionally ubiquitous and atomic substrate that contains and thoroughly permeates all phenomena and noumena. Its defining role is compatibility with what exists. The background can remain present while distinguishable occurrences and unobserved referents also belong to reality.

Ubiquity places this substrate everywhere within the framework. Permeation gives it a relationship to the things it encompasses that goes beyond surrounding them at their outer boundaries. The author describes phenomena and noumena as existing within a background that is also present throughout them.

The word atomic is clarified by the accompanying descriptions of irreducibility and featurelessness. Featurelessness gives the substrate another important role. Phenomena have distinguishable properties; the underlying background is described without such differentiated features. The contrast between those descriptions allows the author to explain a phenomenon as a finite region in which particular characteristics become available to a frame of reference.

The page calls this substrate background nothingness. That phrase combines the idea of an underlying presence with the idea of an absence of differentiated features. Its meaning must therefore be taken from the definitions surrounding it. In the operative framework, background nothingness is the irreducible, featureless condition through which phenomena and noumena are described.

The author says that the phrase could refer to either exclusive or nonexclusive void in general. The description also remains distinct from a claim that the background causally generates phenomena. PHENOMENON is the next formal definition. A phenomenon is a finite region of background nothingness in which a frame of reference renders and observes specific quantifiable features. Those features necessarily establish contrast with the underlying background. The definition combines a bounded occurrence, distinguishable characteristics, and an observing frame.

Finitude makes a phenomenon more limited than the totality of nature or the ubiquitous substrate. Particular phenomena occupy specific regions within the broader account. The definition does not require that the entire encompassing reality be rendered at once for one of its features to become observable.

Rendering gives the frame of reference an active role. The frame detects or presents particular features within its perspective. Observation consequently belongs to a relationship between an agent and what becomes available to it. The document’s later distinction between phenomena and noumena depends on this relationship.

The first example is an electronic light sensor detecting incoming light with a wavelength of exactly 700 nanometers, distinguished from another wavelength such as 470 nanometers. The example supplies a definite measurable contrast. A particular detected feature becomes identifiable through its difference from an alternative.

The second example concerns a human nervous system perceiving the 700-nanometer light and experiencing red. The author connects incoming light, activity in the visual nervous system, and conscious color experience. The example moves from a quantitatively specified input to the qualitative character of an encounter.

The distinction between red and the number 700 is explicitly emphasized. Red names a qualitative experience in the example. Seven hundred names a numerical quantity used to specify wavelength. The document thereby places qualitative and quantitative phenomena within the same broad framework while distinguishing their roles. A useful way to understand this claim is to distinguish a rendered symbol, the quantity represented by it, and a qualitative appearance. The claim is part of the author’s account of abstraction and rendering. The document does not develop a comprehensive theory of mathematical existence. It connects numbers to the hardware and representational activity through which they are instantiated and processed in its examples.

FRAME_OF_REFERENCE is defined as an allocation of background nothingness in which an information processing agent detects the presence or absence of a particular phenomenon at a particular point in space and time. This definition situates the observer within the same encompassing framework as what is observed.

The agent is therefore a localized part of reality. Its observing activity occurs through an allocation of the background, while the phenomena it detects also belong to that background. Observer and observed are related within one totality rather than being assigned wholly separate domains.

Detection of absence is included alongside detection of presence. This allows a frame to register that an expected or specified phenomenon is unavailable at a particular location and time. Such a detection remains a limited relationship within an existing context. It does not establish the absolutely ubiquitous absence required for exclusive void.

A partial frame of reference experiences itself as localized to the present moment within exactly one linear, continuously forward-moving spacetime continuum. The statement describes the author’s model of a limited observer’s temporal situation. That observer encounters a particular present and a succession of moments along its trajectory.

Partiality is significant because the frame does not simultaneously encompass everything. Its current perspective is one allocation within the broader reality. The document represents a frame at time t as R(t). It uses R for the whole perception-memory structure to which the momentary R(t) belongs. That whole is conceptually described as the summation of the frame’s states across the unique, irreducibly small increments of time elapsing according to it.

This is an idealized complete perspective within the document. The page does not identify an observed agent demonstrated to possess it. The omniscient frame serves as a way to conceptualize the totality that partial frames access only locally and successively.

Distinguishing variety from instances helps explain the assertion. NOUMENON is the final formal definition, with noumena identified as its plural. It is defined as a specific phenomenon that is not directly observed by a particular partial frame of reference at a particular point in space and time. The definition is explicitly relative to a frame and a moment.

This is the key to the page’s use of the term. An item can be phenomenal for one observer and noumenal for another. The difference concerns their observational relationships to it. The document gives a simple example involving frames A and B and an item X. A is observing X, while B is not. For A, X is a phenomenon. For B, X is a noumenon. The same referent supports both descriptions within the situation specified.

The pseudocode states these relationships as a true observation condition for A and a false observation condition for B. It is an illustrative notation rather than a program for detecting actual subjective states. Its purpose is to make the relation among observer, object, and classification explicit.

The quotation marks around the wording about B’s experience are meaningful. B does not directly observe X in the example. A false observation condition does not establish that X is absent from reality. The example expressly includes another frame observing it. The distinction allows the author to describe the limits of a perspective without equating those limits with the limits of existence.

The same definition also permits an item’s status to change relative to one frame over time. If an observer later directly encounters something previously unobserved, the relationship changes. The page then distinguishes an unobserved referent from a thought about that referent. An agent registering a thought is experiencing a phenomenon within its own frame. This follows from keeping the two terms separate. The document’s distinction prevents the immediate availability of a representation from being mistaken for direct observation of what it represents.

The recording offers no test that could establish whether reality actually preserves all that ever exists eternally.

For me, the qualifying “if” gives the ending much of its openness. The narrator does not need to resolve that cosmological possibility to finish the thought. The possibility remains available alongside the more concrete statement that an internet archive contains traces of its activity.

I am left considering what a successful hiatus would mean within the terms this recording supplies. It could involve fewer updates, less intensive updates, more time outside, employment, or activities that remain private. Those possibilities are not identical, and the speech does not force them into one definite outcome.

I find the recording most compelling where it permits attachment and change to remain simultaneous. KARBYTES is enjoyable. Maintaining it takes time. The narrator wants continuity and also wants a less continuously documented life. The video preserves that negotiation, including its hesitations, while the camera briefly leaves the room and eventually comes back.
